=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a string s and a pattern string p, where p contains exactly two '*' characters.
The '*' in p matches any sequence of zero or more characters.
Return the length of the shortest substring in s that matches p. If there is no such substring, return -1.
Note: The empty substring is considered valid.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "abaacbaecebce", p = "ba*c*ce"
Output: 8
Explanation:
The shortest matching substring of p in s is "baecebce".

Example 2:

Input: s = "baccbaadbc", p = "cc*baa*adb"
Output: -1
Explanation:
There is no matching substring in s.

Example 3:

Input: s = "a", p = "**"
Output: 0
Explanation:
The empty substring is the shortest matching substring.

Example 4:

Input: s = "madlogic", p = "*adlogi*"
Output: 6
Explanation:
The shortest matching substring of p in s is "adlogi".

 
Constraints:

1 <= s.length <= 10^5
2 <= p.length <= 10^5
s contains only lowercase English letters.
p contains only lowercase English letters and exactly two '*'.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def shortestMatchingSubstring(self, s: str, p: str) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

KMP prefix function:
def prefix_function(pattern):
    pi=[0]*len(pattern)
    for i in range(1,len(pattern)):
        j=pi[i-1]
        while j>0 and pattern[i]!=pattern[j]:
            j=pi[j-1]
        if pattern[i]==pattern[j]:
            j+=1
        pi[i]=j
    return pi

Search:
def find_occurrences(text, pattern):
    if not pattern: return []? But we only call non-empty.
    pi=prefix_function(pattern)
    res=[]
    j=0
    for i,ch in enumerate(text):
        while j>0 and ch != pattern[j]:
            j=pi[j-1]
        if ch == pattern[j]:
            j+=1
        if j == len(pattern):
            res.append(i - len(pattern) + 1)
            j = pi[j-1]
    return res
Need handle len(pattern) maybe 0 not call. Good.

def min_two_part(starts1, len1, starts2, len2):
    # returns min (start2+len2 - start1) over start1 in starts1, start2 in starts2 with start1+len1 <= start2
    ans=inf
    for st1 in starts1:
        idx=bisect_left(starts2, st1+len1)
        if idx < len(starts2):
            ans = min(ans, starts2[idx]+len2 - st1)
    return ans
This handles A then C, A then B, B then C. For A empty B,C nonempty, first=B, second=C. For A nonempty B nonempty C empty, first=A, second=B. For A nonempty B empty C nonempty, first=A, second=C. Good.

For three-part A,B,C nonempty:
def min_three_part(startsA,lenA, startsB,lenB, startsC,lenC):
    ans=inf
    # iterate over A starts, find earliest B, then earliest C
    for sa in startsA:
        idxB = bisect_left(startsB, sa+lenA)
        if idxB == len(startsB): continue
        sb = startsB[idxB]
        idxC = bisect_left(startsC, sb+lenB)
        if idxC == len(startsC): continue
        sc = startsC[idxC]
        ans = min(ans, sc+lenC - sa)
    return ans

Pseudo:

from bisect import bisect_left

class Solution:
    def shortestMatchingSubstring(self, s: str, p: str) -> int:
        parts = p.split('*')
        A,B,C = parts[0], parts[1], parts[2]
        # Since p has exactly two '*', split returns 3 parts. But if p maybe? constraints exactly two, yes.
        def occurrences(pat):
            if not pat:
                return []
            # prefix function
            m = len(pat)
            pi = [0]*m
            j=0
            for i in range(1,m):
                j = pi[i-1]? Wait standard:
                j = pi[i-1]
                while j > 0 and pat[i] != pat[j]:
                    j = pi[j-1]
                if pat[i] == pat[j]:
                    j += 1
                pi[i] = j
            res=[]
            j=0
            for i,ch in enumerate(s):
                while j > 0 and ch != pat[j]:
                    j = pi[j-1]
                if ch == pat[j]:
                    j += 1
                if j == m:
                    res.append(i - m + 1)
                    j = pi[j-1]
            return res

        occA = occurrences(A) if A else []
        occB = occurrences(B) if B else []
        occC = occurrences(C) if C else []

        INF = 10**18
        ans = INF

        # helper two-part: first part then second part
        def two_part(starts1, len1, starts2, len2):
            best = INF
            # maybe optimize with pointer? bisect ok.
            for st1 in starts1:
                need = st1 + len1
                idx = bisect_left(starts2, need)
                if idx < len(starts2):
                    length = starts2[idx] + len2 - st1
                    if length < best:
                        best = length
            return best

        # helper three-part
        def three_part(startsA, lenA, startsB, lenB, startsC, lenC):
            best = INF
            for sa in startsA:
                idxB = bisect_left(startsB, sa + lenA)
                if idxB == len(startsB):
                    continue
                sb = startsB[idxB]
                idxC = bisect_left(startsC, sb + lenB)
                if idxC == len(startsC):
                    continue
                sc = startsC[idxC]
                length = sc + lenC - sa
                if length < best:
                    best = length
            return best

        if A and B and C:
            ans = three_part(occA, len(A), occB, len(B), occC, len(C))
        elif A and B: # C empty
            ans = two_part(occA, len(A), occB, len(B))
        elif A and C: # B empty
            ans = two_part(occA, len(A), occC, len(C))
        elif B and C: # A empty
            ans = two_part(occB, len(B), occC, len(C))
        elif A:
            ans = len(A) if occA else INF
        elif B:
            ans = len(B) if occB else INF
        elif C:
            ans = len(C) if occC else INF
        else:
            ans = 0

        return -1 if ans == INF else ans
